# AWS MLA-C01 exam-prep video extraction

> This Markdown study guide combines timed speech recognition, on-screen OCR, and direct links to sampled video frames. Spoken content is presented as concise answer/explanation cues rather than a word-for-word transcript; the frame links preserve the visual evidence for review. OCR may contain minor recognition errors, especially in small fonts or animated answer overlays.

## How to use this guide

- Start with the question/slide records and try to answer before reading the answer cue.
- Open the linked frame whenever the OCR is uncertain; it is the actual sampled visual and includes diagrams, tables, answer markers, and layout context.
- Repeated question slides and countdown overlays are collapsed when their text is substantially the same.

1) Case Study - Accompany is building a web-based Al application by using Amazon SageMaker. The application will provide the following capabilities and features; ML experimentation, training, a central model registry, model deployment, and model monitoring. The application must ensure secure and isolated use of training data during the ML lifecycle. The training data is stored in Amazon S3. The company needs to use the central model registry to manage different versions of models in the application. Which action will meet this requirement with the LEAST operational overhead? A. Create a separate Amazon Elastic Container Registry (Amazon ECR) repository for each model. B. Use Amazon Elastic Container Registry (Amazon ECR) and unique tags for each model version. C. Use the SageMaker Model Registry and model groups to catalog the models. D. Use the SageMaker Model Registry and unique tags for each model version. Mipa://ahapingpocel. com

**Spoken answer/explanation cue:**

3:35 — for each model, option B, use Amazon elastic container registry and unique tags for each 3:57 — So the right answer here is option C.

### Visual slide 104 — 1:06:51

**Visual frame:** [0000120342.jpg](inspection/scene_4syl_low/0000120342.jpg)

**On-screen extraction:**

Correct option: Switch from the Random Search strategy to the Bayesian Optimization strategy and narrow the range of critical hyperparameters When you're training machine learning models, each dataset and model needs a different set of hyperparameters, which are a kind of variable. The only way to determine these is through multiple experiments, where you pick a set of hyperparameters and tun them through your model. This is called hyperparameter tuning. In essence, you're training your model sequentially with different sets of hyperparameters. This process can be manual, or you can pick one of several automated hyperparameter tuning methods. Bayesian Optimization is a technique based on Bayes’ theorem, which describes the probability of an event occurring related to current knowledge. When this is applied to hyperparameter optimization, the algorithm builds a probabilistic model from a set of hyperparameters that optimizes a specific metric. It uses regression analysis to iteratively choose the best set of hyperparameters. Random Search selects groups of hyperparameters randomly on each iteration. It works well when a relatively small number of the hyperparameters primarily determine the model outcome. Bayesian Optimization is more efficient than Random Search for hyperparameter tuning, especially when dealing with complex models and large hyperparameter spaces. It learns from previous trials to predict the best set of hyperparameters, thus focusing the search more effectively. Narrowing the range of critical hyperparameters can further improve the chances of finding the optimal values, leading to better model convergence and performance. Mtps://shapingpixel com

Explanation: Correct option: Applying multiple layers of security measures including input validation, access controls, and continuous monitoring to address vulnerabilities Architecting a defense-in-depth security approach involves implementing multiple layers of security to protect generative Al applications. This includes input validation to prevent malicious data inputs, strict access controls to limit who can interact with the Al models, and continuous monitoring to detect and respond to security incidents. These measures can help address common vulnerabilities and meet the best practices for securing generative Al applications on AWS. Incorrect options: Implementing a single-layer firewall to block unauthorized access to the Al models ~ While a firewall is an important security measure, relying on a single layer of defense is insufficient for comprehensive security. Defense-in-depth requires multiple, overlapping layers of protection. Relying solely on data encryption to protect the Al training data — Data encryption is crucial for protecting data at rest and in transit, but it does not address other vulnerabilities such as input validation or unauthorized access. A holistic security strategy is needed. Using a single authentication mechanism for all users and services accessing the Al models - Employing a single authentication mechanism is a weak security practice. ### Visual slide 9 — 25:01

**Visual frame:** [0000045036.jpg](inspection/scene_XCXS_all/0000045036.jpg)

**On-screen extraction:**

By configuring VPC endpoints for SageMaker and S3, the company can establish private connections between their VPC and these services, ensuring that data does not traverse the public internet. Utilizing VPC security groups allows for fine- grained control over inbound and outbound traffic to the ML resources, effectively managing network access. This approach enhances security and compliance while maintaining operational efficiency. While IAM policies are essential for managing permissions to AWS resources, they are not designed to control network- level access based on source IP addresses. Network access control is more effectively managed through VPC configurations, such as security groups and network access control lists (ACLs). AWS Network Firewall provides comprehensive network protection but may introduce additional complexity and cost. For controlling access to specific AWS services like SageMaker and $3, VPC endpoints combined with security groups offer a more straightforward and cost-effective solution. AWS Shield is designed to protect against DDoS attacks but does not provide the necessary controls to manage routine network access to specific resources. Controlling network access requires configurations at the VPC level, such as security groups and network ACLs.

A significant decline in the F1 score over time often indicates concept drift, where the statistical properties of the target variable change, which means that the underlying data patterns used for predictions has changed, causing the model's predictions to become less accurate. This misalignment between the model's learned patterns and new data leads to decreased performance metrics like the F1 score. An insufficiently complex model would have shown poor performance from the beginning, not after a period of stability. Data quality issues in the original training data would have caused immediate performance problems, not a delayed decline. Incorrect ground truth labels during baseline calculation would result in an inaccurate baseline metric from the start, not a sudden performance drop after months. https.//shapingpacel com

### Visual slide 27 — 34:22

**Visual frame:** [0000061862.jpg](inspection/scene_XCXS_all/0000061862.jpg)

**On-screen extraction:**

SageMaker's network isolation mode is specifically designed to meet this requirement. It ensures that both training and inference containers are completely isolated from the internet and other network resources. This built-in feature provides the least operational overhead because it is managed directly by SageMaker without requiring complex configurations. Amazon VPC interface endpoints allow private communication between SageMaker and other services within the VPC. However, this does not block access to resources within the VPC, and SageMaker containers would still retain access to VPC resources. This solution does not meet the requirement of full network isolation. While restricting the [AM role and configuring VPC security groups can limit access to specific AWS services or IP addresses, this approach does not guarantee network isolation. It adds significant operational overhead to configure and maintain role permissions and security group rules, and it may still leave gaps in isolation. Running SageMaker containers on EC2 instances with no public IP addresses can limit internet access, but this solution introduces manual overhead and deviates from SageMaker's fully managed service. Additionally, custom route tables add complexity and still may not achieve full network isolation as required in the scenario.

Amazon CloudWatch Metrics and Amazon CloudWatch Logs would allow the ML engineering team to monitor and troubleshoot latency and performance issues in their Amazon SageMaker real-time endpoints, ensuring optimal model performance. Amazon CloudWatch Metrics enables the collection and monitoring of real-time metrics such as CPU utilization, memory usage, and model latency for SageMaker endpoints. This facilitates the identification of performance issues and resource bottlenecks. Amazon CloudWatch Logs captures detailed logs of inference requests and responses, aiding in the analysis and troubleshooting of SageMaker endpoint performance issues. AWS X-Ray is designed for tracing and debugging distributed applications, particularly microservices architectures. It is not directly applicable to monitoring SageMaker endpoints. Amazon CloudWatch Lambda Insights provides performance insights specifically for AWS Lambda functions and is not applicable to SageMaker endpoints. Amazon SageMaker Model Monitor focuses on monitoring data quality and detecting data drift in deployed models. While important, it does not directly address latency and performance monitoring of endpoints.

### Visual slide 41 — 1:04:44

**Visual frame:** [0000116547.jpg](inspection/scene_XCXS_all/0000116547.jpg)

**On-screen extraction:**

Correct option: Switch from the Random Search strategy to the Bayesian Optimization strategy and narrow the range of critical hyperparameters When you're training machine learning models, each dataset and model needs a different set of hyperparameters, which are a kind of variable. The only way to determine these is through multiple experiments, where you pick a set of hyperparameters and tun them through your model. This is called hyperparameter tuning. In essence, you're training your model sequentially with different sets of hyperparameters. This process can be manual, or you can pick one of several automated hyperparameter tuning methods. Bayesian Optimization is a technique based on Bayes’ theorem, which describes the probability of an event occurring related to current knowledge. When this is applied to hyperparameter optimization, the algorithm builds a probabilistic model from a set of hyperparameters that optimizes a specific metric. It uses regression analysis to iteratively choose the best set of hyperparameters. Random Search selects groups of hyperparameters randomly on each iteration. It works well when a relatively small number of the hyperparameters primarily determine the model outcome. Bayesian Optimization is more efficient than Random Search for hyperparameter tuning, especially when dealing with complex models and large hyperparameter spaces. It learns from previous trials to predict the best set of hyperparameters, thus focusing the search more effectively. Narrowing the range of critical hyperparameters can further improve the chances of finding the optimal values, leading to better model convergence and performance. Itps//shapinggéxel. com

By configuring VPC endpoints for SageMaker and S3, the company can establish private connections between their VPC and these services, ensuring that data does not traverse the public internet. Utilizing VPC security groups allows for fine- grained control over inbound and outbound traffic to the ML resources, effectively managing network access. This approach enhances security and compliance while maintaining operational efficiency. While IAM policies are essential for managing permissions to AWS resources, they are not designed to control network- level access based on source IP addresses. Network access control is more effectively managed through VPC configurations, such as security groups and network access control lists (ACLs). AWS Network Firewall provides comprehensive network protection but may introduce additional complexity and cost. For controlling access to specific AWS services like SageMaker and $3, VPC endpoints combined with security groups offer a more straightforward and cost-effective solution. AWS Shield is designed to protect against DDoS attacks but does not provide the necessary controls to manage routine network access to specific resources. The model's complexity was insufficient to capture all patterns in the original training data. B. The original training data contained missing values, leading to data quality issues. C. ### Visual slide 23 — 29:21

**Visual frame:** [0000052848.jpg](inspection/scene_GEP_all/0000052848.jpg)

**On-screen extraction:**

A significant decline in the F1 score over time often indicates concept drift, where the statistical properties of the target variable change, which means that the underlying data patterns used for predictions has changed, causing the model's predictions to become less accurate. This misalignment between the model's learned patterns and new data leads to decreased performance metrics like the F1 score. An insufficiently complex model would have shown poor performance from the beginning, not after a period of stability. Data quality issues in the original training data would have caused immediate performance problems, not a delayed decline. Incorrect ground truth labels during baseline calculation would result in an inaccurate baseline metric from the start, not a sudden performance drop after months.

### Visual slide 32 — 34:22

**Visual frame:** [0000061862.jpg](inspection/scene_GEP_all/0000061862.jpg)

**On-screen extraction:**

SageMaker's network isolation mode is specifically designed to meet this requirement. It ensures that both training and inference containers are completely isolated from the internet and other network resources. This built-in feature provides the least operational overhead because it is managed directly by SageMaker without requiring complex configurations. Amazon VPC interface endpoints allow private communication between SageMaker and other services within the VPC. However, this does not block access to resources within the VPC, and SageMaker containers would still retain access to VPC resources. This solution does not meet the requirement of full network isolation. While restricting the IAM role and configuring VPC security groups can limit access to specific AWS services or IP addresses, this approach does not guarantee network isolation. It adds significant operational overhead to configure and maintain role permissions and security group rules, and it may still leave gaps in isolation. Running SageMaker containers on EC2 instances with no public IP addresses can limit internet access, but this solution introduces manual overhead and deviates from SageMaker's fully managed service. Additionally, custom route tables add complexity and still may not achieve full network isolation as required in the scenario.

### Visual slide 50 — 1:04:44

**Visual frame:** [0000116547.jpg](inspection/scene_GEP_all/0000116547.jpg)

**On-screen extraction:**

Correct option: Switch from the Random Search strategy to the Bayesian Optimization strategy and narrow the range of critical hyperparameters When you're training machine learning models, each dataset and model needs a different set of hyperparameters, which are a kind of variable. The only way to determine these is through multiple experiments, where you pick a set of hyperparameters and run them through your model. This is called hyperparameter tuning. In essence, you're training your model sequentially with different sets of hyperparameters. This process can be manual, or you can pick one of several automated hyperparameter tuning methods. Bayesian Optimization is a technique based on Bayes’ theorem, which describes the probability of an event occurring related to current knowledge. When this is applied to hyperparameter optimization, the algorithm builds a probabilistic model from a set of hyperparameters that optimizes a specific metric. It uses regression analysis to iteratively choose the best set of hyperparameters. Random Search selects groups of hyperparameters randomly on each iteration. It works well when a relatively small number of the hyperparameters primarily determine the model outcome. Bayesian Optimization is more efficient than Random Search for hyperparameter tuning, especially when dealing with complex models and large hyperparameter spaces. It learns from previous trials to predict the best set of hyperparameters, thus focusing the search more effectively. Narrowing the range of critical hyperparameters can further improve the chances of finding the optimal values, leading to better model convergence and performance. hitps://shapingpixel. com

## Accuracy notes

- The first two videos had no YouTube English captions, so their audio was transcribed locally.
- The downloadable caption tracks for the third and fourth links were identical and ended early; local transcription was used to cover the remaining runtime.
- Small or animated slide text can be misread by OCR. When an option, AWS service name, or answer letter matters, verify it against the linked frame and the adjacent audio cue.
